March 2022  |  National Grid
Gas Strategy Vision Report
29
Targeted electrification 
and networked geothermal 
National Grid’s vision embraces our role as a leader 
in evaluating opportunities for non-pipe alternatives, 
including consideration of targeted electrification and 
networked geothermal systems. In specific instances, 
it may be possible to coordinate electrification of heat across 
multiple streets or neighborhoods. The main difference 
between networked geothermal and targeted electrification 
through air-source heat pumps is that the former has better 
winter performance and places lower strain on the electric 
grid. This is because networked geothermal systems 
exchange heat with the ground (which stays at a constant 
temperature below a certain depth) instead of with the 
outside air, which fluctuates more widely. For this reason, 
ground-source heat pumps have higher efficiency and 
lower electric needs. 
This targeted approach can have positive impacts, provided 
that the benefits of avoided gas infrastructure outweigh 
the incremental electric system upgrade costs. While this 
approach has not yet been piloted at scale in the Northeast, 
and there are likely to be a range of regulatory issues, there 
are affordability, safety, and reliability criteria that can guide 
our approach.
For example: 
Reliability and critical loads
Targeted electrification should minimize impact on 
gas network performance. For example, mains that 
supply multiple lower-pressure distribution networks 
or critical customers (e.g. hospitals) would be difficult 
to electrify, while smaller segments that do not serve 
critical system requirements would be easier. 
System economics
Targeted electrification should optimize the economics 
of avoided gas network investment and additional 
electric grid investment. For example, it would be 
preferable to target electrification in areas where 
substantial leak-prone-pipe investment is required, 
rather than in areas where new main has been recently 
installed. Similarly, it should aim to minimize 
significant electric grid investment, for example where 
costs are high or siting issues are acute. In such areas, 
for example, upgrading to hydrogen-readiness is 
likely to be preferable. This optimization would require 
understanding key gas and electric network 
characteristics, for example: capacity ‘headroom’ 
on the electric system, cost of electric upgrades in 
different parts of the network, cost and expected timing 
of pipe replacement in different parts of the network, 
and customer density (e.g. meters per mile) as a proxy 
for customer contributions to system investment. 
In sum, a paradigm shift toward whole system 
optimization is likely to be required, to minimize 
costs on the path to net zero.
Customer economics and practicality
Targeted electrification should minimize customer cost 
and disruption. This could be achieved by focusing 
on areas with a prevalence of building types that 
can be electrified at relatively lower cost (e.g. newer, 
detached homes) and where customer disruption 
could be minimized.

Panel
Program
Description
How This Program Will Reduce Emissions
Gas System Reinforcement- Belmont 
Gate Station
This project rebuilds the regulator station to increase 
capacity to maintain pressure above system minimum on 
the gas network during periods of peak demand. 
Replacing old and aging station equipment with 
new equipment and technologies should reduce 
emissions. 
Corrosion
This program is annual corrosion work on the Company's gas 
system. Cathodic protection is installed on the pipe. 
Cathodic protection increases the pipe's useful life, 
reducing future emissions associated with leaks. 
Remote Controlled Valve (RCV)
This program will install RCV on select pipe segments. 
In the event of an incident on an RCV-equipped 
seqment, the RCV will reduce the time of gas 
blowing to atmosphere as the result of third party 
or other damage. The ability to control these events 
remotely will also reduce the number of truck rolls 
required to respond to an incident. 
Main Replacement- Reactive- 
Maintenance
This program will replace sections of main segments and 
associated services identified during leak repairs that leak 
clamps could not repair. 
This program will reduce fugitive emissions from 
leaking connections, pipe damage, or faulty 
equipment. 
Automatic Meter Reading (AMR)
This program includes the purchase and installation of AMR 
equipment to support KEDLI's mandated Meter 
Test/Replacement program. 
AMR allows the Company to remote read meters 
and reduces GHG emissions by reducing vehicle 
trips to read meters. 
Pipeline Integrity- IMP
This program enables select transmission pipes to be 
enabled to use in-line inspection (ILI) technology. This will 
allow for the enhanced operational safety of the Company's 
transmission system. 
This is a mandated project, and also has the 
potential to avoid future leaks and failures on the 
system and therefore tends to reduce GHG 
reductions. 
Pipeline Integrity-IVP
This program will replace pipe with insufficient records. 
This project will proactively review assets and has 
the potential to avoid future leaks and failures on 
the system and therefore tends to reduce GHG 
reductions.  
Service Replacements
This program is non-discretionary work randomly generated 
through public leak reports, programmed leak surveys, 
mandated activities, and customer generated requests. 
This program will reduce fugitive emissions from 
leaking connections, pipe damage, or faulty 
equipment. 
Transmission Station Integrity
This program will repair or replace transmission stations or 
parts at the transmission stations. 
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions by 
replacing old, antiquated equipment with new and 
more reliable equipment. 
System Automation & Control
This program will install Remote Terminal Units (RTUs), 
which will provide temperature, pressure, and flow data 
back to the Gas Control Room. 
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions by 
replacing old, antiquated equipment with new and 
more reliable equipment. 
Gas System Control
This program supports necessary upgrades to the Gas 
Control Room.
This project will proactively review assets and has 
the potential to avoid future leaks and failures on 
the system and therefore tends to reduce GHG 
reductions.  
Heater Installation Program
Gas heaters are designed to safely heat gas to mitigate 
problems caused by low gas temperatures. 
Newly installed heater equipment will perform 
more efficiently than existing equipment and may 
result in nominal emissions reductions. 
Instrumentation and Regulation (I&R)-
Reactive
This program funds the capital repairs of deficiencies at 
pressure regulating facilities found during inspection and 
maintenance. 
This program will reduce fugitive emissions from 
leaking connections, pipe damage, or faulty 
equipment. 
Main Protection- Storm Hardening
This program installs storm hardening measures, including 
ultrasonic meters and valves, to mitigate the public risk 
caused by flood damages to the gas equipment and service 
regulator. 
The valve will provide the ability to remotely 
shutoff the flow of gas to services which will stop 
gas leaks located after the valve more quickly than 
previously possible. 
Proactive Regulator Stations
Pressure regulating stations are repaired or replaced. 
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions by 
replacing old, antiquated equipment with new and 
more reliable equipment. 
Transmission Mains- Greenpoint
This project will replace a 16x12 tee and replace with a 
straight piece of pipe to avoid possible liquids from pooling 
in the spur. 
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions by 
replacing old, antiquated equipment with new and 
more reliable equipment. 
Transmission Service Integrity
This program will ensure the integrity of the transmission 
customer services.  
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions by 
replacing old, antiquated equipment with new and 
more reliable equipment. 
Telecomm- Reactive
This program funds capital repairs and replacements of 
telecom equipment and installations. 
This program helps provide for effective and 
reliable long distance communication services so 
that field information can be obtained without 
personnel travel, reducing emissions associated 
with additional truck rolls.

Infrastructure Programs

Minimizing the impact and duration of emerging 
gas leaks will reduce methane emissions. 
Advanced Leak Detection Program
This program surveys service territories for possible high 
emitting leaks and uses this information to prioritize high 
emitting leaks for repair. 
Helps reduce system methane leaks. 
Inside Service Line Inspections 
Program
This program inspects pipes within buildings. 
Helps the Companies identify and repair gas leaks. 
Clean Energy 2.0
This program will support the Companies' energy efficiency 
programs by replacing an older, more inefficient software 
system. 
This program will allow the Companies manage the 
energy efficiency programs more effectively and 
efficiently. 
Green Button Connect
This program will enable increased access to products and 
services to energy data. 
Will facilitate customer sharing of energy usage 
information to enhance overall energy savings.

Exhibit __ (CLCPA-4) 
Schedule 2
Page 1 of 2
Panel
List of Programs
Description
How This Program Will Impact Emissions
Corrosion
This program is annual corrosion work on the 
Company's gas system. Cathodic protection is 
installed on the pipe to help prevent corrosion. 
Cathodic protection increases the pipe's useful 
life, reducing future emissions associated with 
leaks. 
Remote Controlled Valve (RCV)
This program will install RCV on select pipe 
segments. 
In the event of an incident on an RCV-equipped 
seqment, the RCV will reduce the time of gas 
blowing to atmosphere as the result of third 
party or other damage. The ability to control 
these events remotely will also reduce the 
number of truck rolls required to respond to an 
incident. 
Main Replacement- Reactive- 
Maintenance
This program will replace sections of main 
segments and associated services identified 
during leak repairs that leak clamps could not 
repair. 
This program will reduce fugitive emissions from 
leaking connections, pipe damage, or faulty 
equipment. 
Advanced Meter Reading (AMR)
This program includes the purchase and 
installation of AMR equipment to support 
KEDLI's mandated Meter Test/Replacement 
program. 
AMR allows the Company to remotely read 
meters and reduces GHG emissions by reducing 
vehicle trips to read meters. 
Pipeline Integrity-IVP
This program will replace pipe with insufficient 
records. 
This project will proactively review assets and 
has the potential to avoid future leaks and 
failures on the system and therefore tends to 
reduce GHG reductions.  
Integrity- Meter Pitts
This program will repair and remediate gas 
piping components, regulators, and meters 
exposed to atmospheric conditions. 
This program will remediate current asset 
conditions where future leaks could occur.
Service Replacement
This program is non-discretionary work 
randomly generated through public leak reports, 
programmed leak surveys, mandated activities, 
and customer generated requests. 
This program will reduce fugitive emissions from 
leaking connections, pipe damage, or faulty 
equipment. 
Take Station
This program will install over pressurization 
protection at select take stations. 
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions 
by replacing old, antiquated equipment with new 
and more reliable equipment. 
Gas System Control
This program supports necessary upgrades to 
the Gas Control Room.
This project will proactively review assets and 
has the potential to avoid future leaks and 
failures on the system and therefore tends to 
reduce GHG reductions.  
Transmission Station Integrity
This program will repair or replace transmission 
stations or parts at the transmission stations. 
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions 
by replacing old, antiquated equipment with new 
and more reliable equipment. 
Heater Installation Program
This program will install gas heaters, which are 
designed to safely heat gas to mitigate problems 
caused by low gas temperatures. 
Newly installed heater equipment will perform 
more efficiently than existing equipment and 
may result in nominal emissions reductions. 
Instrumentation and Regulation 
(I&R)- Reactive
This program funds the capital repairs of 
deficiencies at pressure regulating facilities 
found during inspection and maintenance. 
This program will reduce fugitive emissions from 
leaking connections, pipe damage, or faulty 
equipment. 
Main Protection- Storm Hardening This program installs storm hardening measures, 
including ultrasonic meters and valves, to 
mitigate the public risk caused by flood damages 
to the gas equipment and service regulator. 
The valve will provide the ability to remotely 
shutoff the flow of gas to services which will stop 
gas leaks located after the valve more quickly 
than previously possible. 
Proactive Regulator Stations
Pressure regulating stations are repaired or 
replaced. 
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions 
by replacing old, antiquated equipment with new 
and more reliable equipment. 
GIOP
KEDLI Programs with Non-Quantified Emissions Benefits


--- Page 131 ---
Exhibit __ (CLCPA-4) 
Schedule 2
Page 2 of 2
Transmission Service Integrity
This program will ensure the integrity of the 
transmission customer services.  
This project will indirectly reduce GHG emissions 
by replacing old, antiquated equipment with new 
and more reliable equipment. 
Telecom- Reactive
This program funds capital repairs and 
replacements of telecom equipment and 
installations. 
This program helps provide for effective and 
reliable long distance communication services so 
that field information can be obtained without 
personnel travel, reducing emissions associated 
with additional truck rolls. 
Economic Development and the 
Future of Heat
This program invests in technologies that enable 
customers to be more efficient and productive 
while contributing to New York's ongoing energy 
transformation. 
This program invests in projects that involve non-
pipeline alternatives to traditional natural gas 
delivery. 
Sustainable Gas and Economic 
Development
This program will fund projects that 
demonstrate sustainable gas technologies
This program funds projects that could reduce 
methane emissions from naturally occurring 
sources (landfills, waste water treatment plants, 
farms, etc.) . 
Weatherization Health and Safety
This program will enable remediation of health 
and safety barriers to weatherization, such as 
carbon monoxide hazards, mold, insufficient 
wiring or ventilation, etc. 
This program will encourage more 
weatherization projects, which reduce 
emissions, in Disadvantaged Communities. 
Disadvantaged Community 
Outreach
This program identifies key barriers to 
engagement in energy efficiency programs and 
strategies to overcome those barriers, while 
identifying the most effective ways to promote 
the energy efficiency programs. 
This program will increase participation by 
customers in Disadvantaged Communities in 
energy efficiency programs, which will reduce 
emissions. 
Energy Efficiency Language Access 
Program
This program provides a mix of language 
accessibility service to apply to websites, flyers, 
advertisements, and other customer facing 
materials. 
This program will remove a significant barrier to 
customer participation in energy efficiency 
programs and should increase participation. 
Digital Building Replica Pilot (DAC 
School Building)
This program creates a data model of a school 
building that functions identically to a the 
physical building being modeled.  
The model will be created to predict energy 
savings from participation in the Companies' 
energy efficiency programs and drive increased 
participation. 
Clean Energy Advisors
This program will help customers navigate the 
various clean energy programs. 
This program will help customers, particularly 
LMI customers reduce their emissions through 
participation in clean energy programs 
CLCPA
Gas Low and Moderate Income 
EAM
This metric encourages the Companies to exceed 
the LMI EE targets set by NE:NY by finding 
innovative ways to achieve more energy savings. 
Exceeding the LMI EE targets will reduce GHG 
emissions beyond expected GHG emission 
reductions in NE:NY. 
Advanced Communications 
Infrastructure Programs
This program provides real time gas system 
feedback to allow for more effective emergency 
response and potentially minimize the impact 
and duration of emerging leaks. 
Minimizing the impact and duration of emerging 
gas leaks will reduce methane emissions. 
Advanced Leak Detection Program This program surveys service territories for 
possible high emitting leaks and uses this 
information to prioritize high emitting leaks for 
repair. 
Helps reduce system methane leaks. 
Inside Service Line Inspections 
Program
This program inspects pipes within buildings. 
Helps the Companies identify and repair gas 
leaks. 
Clean Energy 2.0
This program will support the Companies' energy 
efficiency programs by replacing an older, more 
inefficient software system. 
This program will allow the Companies manage 
the energy efficiency programs more effectively 
and efficiently. 
Green Button Connect
This program will enable increased access to 
products and services to energy data. 
Will facilitate customer sharing of energy usage 
information to enhance overall energy savings. 
Additional information on the listed programs is provided in the testimony of the respective indicated panel.

Customer
Gas Safety

KEDNY and KEDLI Rate Case Proposals 
Supporting Disadvantaged Communities 
Disadvantaged Communities Benefits 
Significant proportion of LPP located in Disadvantaged 
Communities, and retiring such facilities will promote 
safety and improve local air quality. 
Proposed EV roll out located in and near Disadvantaged 
Communities, reducing local ambient noise, improving air 
quality, and reducing GHG emissions. 
To use an overall design that improves local air quality 
and mitigates potential adverse environmental factors 
that affect Disadvantaged Communities in the area. 
Will help address prerequisite home conditions to enable 
LMI customers and customers in Disadvantaged 
Communities to access weatherization services more 
equitably because weatherization often involves 
significant home construction projects. 
Multiple strategies will engage customers within 
Disadvantaged Communities to participate in energy 
efficiency programs. 
Language accessibility services will be applied to a variety 
of customer-facing public information in focused effort to 
increase participation by non-English speaking customers 
in energy savings programs. 
Will enable schools in Disadvantaged Communities to 
understand the energy benefits of different energy-
savings measures without, or in advance of, hiring an 
external vendor, to make more cost-effective decisions. 
The economic development programs are being re-
framed to deliver greater benefits to Disadvantaged 
Communities, including adjusting eligibility requirements 
re prioritizing to provide additional funding for eligible 
projects located in Disadvantaged Communities. 
Encourages the Companies to exceed on the LMI EE 
targets set by NE:NYand to direct EE savings towards 
Disadvantaged Communities in support of the 35% 
benefits target established in the CLCPA. 
Would drive greater EAP participation by providing a bill 
discount to more income eligible customers. Encourages 
the Companies to increase investment and identify and 
advance innovative approaches to identify, reach, and 
enroll customers in EAP.

GSTS performance and associated incentives will be calculated by determining: (i) a baseline energy
3
efficiency (“EE”) unit cost savings; (ii) the difference between that baseline and the actual acquired
4
EE unit cost savings; and (iii) applying a percent share (30%) to the result. The formula is as
5
follows:
6
7
𝑆𝑇𝑆𝐸𝐴𝑀($) =
8
(𝐶𝑌𝑥𝐵𝑎𝑠𝑒𝑙𝑖𝑛𝑒𝐿𝑀𝑀𝐵𝑡𝑢𝑈𝑛𝑖𝑡𝐶𝑜𝑠𝑡– 𝐶𝑌𝑥𝐴𝑐𝑞𝑢𝑖𝑟𝑒𝑑𝐿𝑀𝑀𝐵𝑡𝑢𝑈𝑛𝑖𝑡𝐶𝑜𝑠𝑡) ∗𝐶𝑌𝑥𝐴𝑐𝑞𝑢𝑖𝑟𝑒𝑑𝐿𝑀𝑀𝐵𝑡𝑢* 30%
9
10
where:
11
x = 1, 2, 3, etc. for Calendar Year 1 (2024), Calendar Year 2 (2025), Calendar Year 3 (2026), etc.
12
13
CYx Baseline LMMBtu (“lifetime MMBtu”) Unit Cost = 𝐶𝑌𝑥𝐵𝑎𝑠𝑒𝑙𝑖𝑛𝑒𝐵𝑢𝑑𝑔𝑒𝑡
14
𝐶𝑌𝑥𝐵𝑎𝑠𝑒𝑙𝑖𝑛𝑒𝐿𝑀𝑀𝐵𝑡𝑢
15
16
CYx Baseline Budget = The Company’s total non-LMI energy efficiency budgets approved in
17
the January 2020 New Efficiency:New York (“NE:NY”) Order
18
19
CYx Baseline LMMBtu = CYx Baseline AMMBtu * Portfolio EUL
20
21
CYx Baseline AMMBtu (“annual MMBtu”) = The Company’s non-LMI gas energy
22
annual savings target approved in the January 2020 NE:NY Order
23
24
Portfolio EUL = The Company’s portfolio-level Effective Useful Life in the year prior
25
to CYx, as determined by the applicable Technical Resources Manual (“TRM”), and
26
utilizing a verified gross savings methodology2
27
28
CYx Acquired LMMBtu Unit Cost = 𝐶𝑌𝑥𝐴𝑐𝑡𝑢𝑎𝑙𝐸𝑥𝑝𝑒𝑛𝑑𝑖𝑡𝑢𝑟𝑒𝑠
29
𝐶𝑌𝑥𝐴𝑐𝑞𝑢𝑖𝑟𝑒𝑑𝐿𝑀𝑀𝐵𝑡𝑢
30
31
CYx Actual Expenditures = The Company’s total expenditures on its gas energy efficiency
32
portfolio in CYx exclusive of LMI programs
33
34
CYx Acquired LMMBtu = CYx Acquired AMMBtu * CYx Portfolio EUL
35
36
CYx Acquired AMMBtu = The Company’s acquired annual verified gross gas
37
energy savings, exclusive of savings from LMI programs, as reported in the
38
Company’s year-end scorecard
39
40
CYx Portfolio EUL = The Company’s portfolio EUL, as determined by the
41
applicable TRM at the time the savings are acquired, and utilizing a verified gross
42
savings methodology
43
44
LMI programs = the portfolio of programs established in the Statewide Implementation plan
45
46
This EAM is computed formulaically, per the Metric above, and as such has no minimum, midpoint,
47
or maximum targets.
48
49
Achievement is based on the formula detailed in the  section above. To account for the
50
shifting of funds between years, the threshold for qualifying to collect the GSTS EAM shall be each
51
year’s NE:NY annual savings target (excluding LMI programs) plus the cumulative over- or underperformance
52
the NE:NY period, as follows:
53
54
𝑇ℎ𝑟𝑒𝑠ℎ𝑜𝑙𝑑= (𝑂𝑣𝑒𝑟/𝑢𝑛𝑑𝑒𝑟𝑝𝑒𝑟𝑓𝑜𝑟𝑚𝑎𝑛𝑐𝑒𝐶𝑌𝑥−1 + 𝑂𝑣𝑒𝑟/𝑢𝑛𝑑𝑒𝑟𝑝𝑒𝑟𝑓𝑜𝑟𝑚𝑎𝑛𝑐𝑒𝐶𝑌𝑥−2 + …) ÷ (2026 − 𝐶𝑌𝑥)
55
56
where:
57
𝑂𝑣𝑒𝑟/underperformance = 𝐴𝑐𝑡𝑢𝑎𝑙𝑎𝑛𝑛𝑢𝑎𝑙𝑀𝑀𝐵𝑡𝑢𝑠𝑎𝑣𝑖𝑛𝑔𝑠𝑎𝑐ℎ𝑖𝑒𝑣𝑒𝑑𝑣𝑖𝑎𝑡ℎ𝑒𝐶𝑜𝑚𝑝𝑎𝑛𝑦’𝑠𝑚𝑎𝑟𝑘𝑒𝑡-𝑟𝑎𝑡𝑒
58
𝑒𝑛𝑒𝑟𝑔𝑦𝑒𝑓𝑓𝑖𝑐𝑖𝑒𝑛𝑐𝑦𝑝𝑜𝑟𝑡𝑓𝑜𝑙𝑖𝑜− 𝐴𝑛𝑛𝑢𝑎𝑙𝑀𝑀𝐵𝑡𝑢𝑚𝑎𝑟𝑘𝑒𝑡-𝑟𝑎𝑡𝑒𝑎𝑛𝑛𝑢𝑎𝑙𝑠𝑎𝑣𝑖𝑛𝑔𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑟𝑔𝑒𝑡𝑎𝑠𝑠𝑒𝑡𝑖𝑛𝑡ℎ𝑒𝑁𝐸: 𝑁𝑌𝑂𝑟𝑑𝑒𝑟
59
and where the first CY in which over/underperformance is measured is 2019. If the Commission authorizes
60
additional budgets and targets, the EAM calculation, including threshold shall be updated accordingly.
